I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We need to calculate the weight of a word where each letter has a weight:
   - a = 1, b = 2, c = 3, ..., z = 26

2. Prof. M skips some letters, and those skipped letters contribute 0 to the weight
3. The final weight is the sum of weights of all characters in the word, minus the weights of skipped letters that appear in the word

4. Key points:
   - We have a word W
   - We have N letters that are skipped
   - For each skipped letter that appears in the word, we subtract its weight (1 to 26)

## Step-by-Step Solution

### Step 1: Understand the calculation
- Original weight of word = sum of positions (a=1, b=2, etc.) of all characters
- Skipped letters contribute 0 instead of their actual weight
- So we subtract the weight of each skipped letter that appears in the word

### Step 2: Algorithm
1. For each test case:
   - Read the word W
   - Read the number of skipped letters N
   - Read the N skipped letters
   - Calculate the original weight of the word (sum of positions of each character)
   - For each skipped letter, if it appears in the word, subtract its position value (a=1, b=2, etc.)
   - Output the final weight

### Step 3: Implementation details
- Convert letter to weight: ord(char) - ord('a') + 1
- Create a set of skipped letters for O(1) lookup
- Iterate through each character in the word, add its weight to total
- For each character in the word, if it's in the skipped set, subtract its weight

Wait, let me reconsider. Actually, we can do it more efficiently:
- Total weight = sum of weights of all characters in word
- For each skipped letter that appears in the word, subtract the weight of that letter

### Step 4: Code Implementation

```python
def get_letter_weight(char):
    return ord(char) - ord('a') + 1

t = int(input())

for _ in range(t):
    word = input().strip()
    n = int(input())
    skipped_letters = input().strip().split()
    
    # Convert skipped letters to set for O(1) lookup
    skipped_set = set(skipped_letters)
    
    # Calculate total weight of the word
    total_weight = 0
    for char in word:
        weight = get_letter_weight(char)
        total_weight += weight
    
    # Subtract weight of each skipped letter that appears in the word
    for skipped_char in skipped_letters:
        weight = get_letter_weight(skipped_char)
        # If this skipped letter appears in the word, subtract its weight
        # We need to check if this letter appears in the word
        if skipped_char in word:
            total_weight -= weight
    
    print(total_weight)
```

Wait, I need to be more careful. Let me trace through the examples:

Example 1: "abcd", skip "a", "c"
- Original weight: a(1) + b(2) + c(3) + d(4) = 10
- Skip 'a': subtract 1, skip 'c': subtract 3
- Result: 10 - 1 - 3 = 6 ✓

Example 2: "aaaa", skip "a"
- Original weight: a(1) + a(1) + a(1) + a(1) = 4
- Skip 'a': appears in word, subtract weight 1
- But we have 4 a's, so we need to subtract 4 * 1 = 4
- Result: 4 - 4 = 0 ✓

I made a mistake in my algorithm. For each occurrence of a skipped letter, we need to subtract its weight.

Actually, my current algorithm already handles this because we're checking if the skipped letter exists in the word, and if it does, we subtract its weight once.

But that's not right - if a letter is skipped and appears multiple times, we need to subtract its weight for each occurrence.

Let me fix this:

```python
def get_letter_weight(char):
    return ord(char) - ord('a') + 1

t = int(input())

for _ in range(t):
    word = input().strip()
    n = int(input())
    skipped_letters = input().strip().split()
    
    # Convert skipped letters to set for O(1) lookup
    skipped_set = set(skipped_letters)
    
    # Calculate total weight of the word
    total_weight = 0
    for char in word:
        weight = get_letter_weight(char)
        total_weight += weight
    
    # Subtract weight of each skipped letter that appears in the word
    # For each skipped letter, if it appears in the word, subtract its weight for each occurrence
    for char in word:
        if char in skipped_set:
            total_weight -= get_letter_weight(char)
    
    print(total_weight)
```

This looks better. For each character in the word, if it's in the skipped set, we subtract its weight.

Let me verify with examples:

Example 1: "abcd", skip "a", "c"
- Original: 1+2+3+4 = 10
- For 'a' (in skipped): subtract 1
- For 'b' (not skipped): no change  
- For 'c' (in skipped): subtract 3
- For 'd' (not skipped): no change
- Result: 10 - 1 - 3 = 6 ✓

Example 2: "aaaa", skip "a"
- Original: 1+1+1+1 = 4
- For each 'a': subtract 1
- So subtract 4 times: 4 - 4 = 0 ✓

Example 3: "ababab", skip "a", "b"
- Original: 1+2+1+2+1+2 = 9
- For each 'a': subtract 1, there are 3 a's → subtract 3
- For each 'b': subtract 2, there are 3 b's → subtract 6
- Total subtract: 3 + 6 = 9
- Result: 9 - 9 = 0 ✓

Perfect! This matches the expected output.

```python
def get_letter_weight(char):
    return ord(char) - ord('a') + 1

t = int(input())

for _ in range(t):
    word = input().strip()
    n = int(input())
    skipped_letters = input().strip().split()
    
    # Convert skipped letters to set for O(1) lookup
    skipped_set = set(skipped_letters)
    
    # Calculate total weight of the word
    total_weight = 0
    for char in word:
        weight = get_letter_weight(char)
        total_weight += weight
    
    # Subtract weight of each skipped letter that appears in the word
    for char in word:
        if char in skipped_set:
            total_weight -= get_letter_weight(char)
    
    print(total_weight)
```

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We need to calculate the weight of a word where each letter has a specific weight:
   - a = 1, b = 2, c = 3, ..., z = 26

2. Prof. M skips some letters, and those skipped letters contribute 0 to the final weight.

3. The final weight is the sum of weights of all letters in the word, minus the weights of all skipped letters.

4. We need to:
   - Read the number of test cases
   - For each test case, read the word, number of skipped letters, and the skipped letters
   - Calculate the total weight of the word and subtract the weights of skipped letters

## Step-by-Step Solution

### Step 1: Analyze the examples
- Example 1: "abcd" with skipped letters "a,c"
  - Original weight: a(1) + b(2) + c(3) + d(4) = 10
  - Skipped letters: a(1) + c(3) = 4
  - Result: 10 - 4 = 6

- Example 2: "aaaa" with skipped letter "a"
  - Original weight: a(1) + a(1) + a(1) + a(1) = 4
  - Skipped letters: a(1) = 1
  - Result: 4 - 1 = 3... wait, but expected output is 0.

Let me re-examine the problem. Looking at the sample output, "aaaa" with skipped "a" gives 0.

Ah, I see! The problem says "every characters which are skipped by Prof. M take its weight as 0". This means we only consider the letters that are NOT skipped.

But let me check the second example again: "aaaa" with skip "a" should give 0 because all letters are skipped.

Actually, rereading: "those letters which are skipped by Prof. M, take its weight as 0". This seems to mean that if a letter is skipped, its contribution is 0.

So for "aaaa" with skip "a", since all letters are skipped (a), the result should be 0.

Wait, the problem says "he skips some of its letters" - so if the word contains letters that he skips, those letters contribute 0.

For "aaaa" with skip "a": all occurrences of 'a' are skipped, so all contribute 0 → total weight = 0.

For "ababab" with skip "a,b": all letters are skipped, so total weight = 0.

### Step 2: Algorithm
1. For each test case:
   - Read the word W
   - Read N (number of skipped letters)
   - Read N skipped letters
   - Create a set of skipped letters for O(1) lookup
   - Calculate total weight = sum of weights of characters in W that are NOT in skipped set
   - Output the total weight

### Step 3: Implementation

```python
def get_char_weight(char):
    return ord(char) - ord('a') + 1

t = int(input())

for _ in range(t):
    word = input().strip()
    n = int(input())
    skipped_letters = input().strip().split()
    
    # Convert skipped letters to set for O(1) lookup
    skipped_set = set(skipped_letters)
    
    # Calculate total weight
    total_weight = 0
    for char in word:
        if char not in skipped_set:
            total_weight += get_char_weight(char)
    
    print(total_weight)
```

- Total: 2 + 4 = 6 ✓

Example 2: "aaaa", skip "a"
- All a's are in skip → all skipped
- Total: 0 ✓

Example 3: "ababab", skip "a,b"
- All letters are in skip → all skipped
- Total: 0 ✓

Model Response

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We need to calculate the "weight" of a word where each character has a value:
   - a = 1, b = 2, c = 3, ..., z = 26

2. Prof. M skips some letters, and those skipped letters contribute 0 to the total weight.

3. The total weight is the sum of weights of all characters in the word, but only for the characters that are NOT skipped.

4. The formula: Total weight = sum of (character position) for all characters that are NOT skipped.

## Step-by-step Solution

### Step 1: Understand the input structure
- First line: number of test cases T
- For each test case:
  - First line: word W
  - Second line: N (number of skipped letters)
  - Third line: N skipped letters (space-separated)

### Step 2: Algorithm
1. For each test case:
   - Read the word W
   - Read N (number of skipped letters)
   - Read the skipped letters and store them in a set for O(1) lookup
   - Calculate the total weight by iterating through each character in W
   - For each character, if it's NOT in the skipped set, add its position value (a=1, b=2, etc.)
   - Print the total weight

### Step 3: Implementation details
- Character value = ord(char) - ord('a') + 1
- Use a set to store skipped letters for fast lookup
- Sum up only the characters that are not skipped

### Step 4: Trace through examples

**Example 1: "abcd", skip "a", "c"**
- a: skip (value 1, not included)
- b: not skip (value 2, included)
- c: skip (value 3, not included) 
- d: not skip (value 4, included)
- Total = 2 + 4 = 6 ✓

**Example 2: "aaaa", skip "a"**
- All a's are skipped, so total = 0 ✓

**Example 3: "ababab", skip "a", "b"**
- All characters are skipped, so total = 0 ✓

## Code Implementation

```python
# Read number of test cases
t = int(input())

for _ in range(t):
    # Read the word
    word = input().strip()
    
    # Read number of skipped letters
    n = int(input())
    
    # Read skipped letters and store in set
    skipped_letters = set(input().split())
    
    # Calculate total weight
    total_weight = 0
    for char in word:
        # If character is not skipped, add its weight
        if char not in skipped_letters:
            char_weight = ord(char) - ord('a') + 1
            total_weight += char_weight
    
    # Print the result
    print(total_weight)
```

The solution works as follows:
1. Read T test cases
2. For each test case, read the word, number of skipped letters, and the actual skipped letters
3. Convert skipped letters to a set for O(1) lookup
4. Iterate through each character in the word and add its weight only if it's not in the skipped set
5. Output the total weight

Time complexity: O(|W|) per test case
Space complexity: O(N) for storing skipped letters

Model Response

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We need to calculate the weight of a word where each character has a weight:
   - a = 1, b = 2, c = 3, ..., z = 26

2. Prof. M skips some letters, and those skipped letters contribute 0 to the final weight

3. The final weight = sum of weights of all characters in the word - sum of weights of skipped letters that appear in the word

4. We need to handle multiple test cases

## Step-by-Step Solution

### Step 1: Identify what we need to do
- For each word, calculate the total weight of all characters
- Identify which characters were skipped
- Subtract the weights of skipped characters that appear in the word
- Return the resulting weight

### Step 2: Break down the algorithm
1. Read number of test cases
2. For each test case:
   - Read the word
   - Read number of skipped letters N
   - Read the N skipped letters
   - Calculate total weight of the word (sum of positions of each character)
   - Calculate weight of skipped characters that appear in the word
   - Result = total weight - skipped characters weight

### Step 3: Calculate character weights
- For a character c, its weight = ord(c) - ord('a') + 1

### Step 4: Implementation

```python
def solve():
    t = int(input())
    
    for _ in range(t):
        word = input().strip()
        n = int(input())
        skipped_letters = input().strip().split()
        
        # Calculate total weight of the word
        total_weight = 0
        for char in word:
            total_weight += ord(char) - ord('a') + 1
        
        # Calculate weight of skipped characters that appear in the word
        skipped_weight = 0
        for skipped_char in skipped_letters:
            # Check if this skipped character appears in the word
            for char in word:
                if char == skipped_char:
                    skipped_weight += ord(skipped_char) - ord('a') + 1
                    break
        
        # Result is total weight minus weight of skipped characters
        result = total_weight - skipped_weight
        print(result)

solve()
```

## Verification with Sample Input

Test case 1: "abcd", skip "a", "c"
- Total weight: a(1) + b(2) + c(3) + d(4) = 10
- Skipped characters: a(1) and c(3) appear in word
- Skipped weight: 1 + 3 = 4
- Result: 10 - 4 = 6 ✓

Test case 2: "aaaa", skip "a"
- Total weight: a(1) * 4 = 4
- Skipped characters: a(1) appears in word
- Skipped weight: 1
- Result: 4 - 1 = 3... Wait, this doesn't match expected output of 0.

Let me re-read the problem...

Actually, looking at the sample output, "aaaa" with skip "a" gives 0. This means that if a letter is skipped, ALL occurrences of that letter are valued as 0.

So the problem is: when a letter is skipped, it contributes 0 to the total weight, regardless of how many times it appears.

So the corrected algorithm:
1. For each character in the word:
   - If the character is skipped, contribute 0
   - Otherwise, contribute its weight (position in alphabet)

```python
t = int(input())

for _ in range(t):
    word = input().strip()
    n = int(input())
    skipped_letters = input().strip().split()
    
    # Convert skipped letters to a set for O(1) lookup
    skipped_set = set(skipped_letters)
    
    # Calculate total weight - only count characters that are not skipped
    total_weight = 0
    for char in word:
        if char not in skipped_set:
            total_weight += ord(char) - ord('a') + 1
    
    print(total_weight)
```

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We need to calculate the weight of a word where each character has a weight:
   - a = 1, b = 2, c = 3, ..., z = 26

2. Prof. M skips some letters, and those skipped letters contribute 0 to the final weight

3. The final weight = sum of weights of all characters in the word MINUS the weights of skipped letters that appear in the word

4. For each test case:
   - Input a word W
   - Input N (number of letters skipped)
   - Input N letters that are skipped

## Step-by-Step Solution

### Step 1: Calculate total weight of the word
- For each character in the word, add its weight (a=1, b=2, etc.)

### Step 2: Calculate weight of skipped letters that appear in the word
- For each skipped letter, if it appears in the word, add its weight to the loss
- If a letter is skipped but doesn't appear in the word, it contributes 0

### Step 3: Final weight = total weight - loss from skipped letters

### Step 4: Implementation details
- Convert character to weight: `ord(char) - ord('a') + 1`
- Store skipped letters in a set for O(1) lookup
- Iterate through each character in the word
- Add the weight of each character to total if it's not skipped
- Subtract the weight of skipped characters that appear in the word

Wait, let me reconsider the problem. The problem states that "letters which are skipped by Prof. M take its weight as 0". This means:
- If a character is skipped, its contribution is 0
- If a character is not skipped, its contribution is its normal weight

So the algorithm is:
1. Calculate sum of weights for all characters in the word
2. Subtract the weights of all characters that are skipped (since they contribute 0)

But we need to be careful: only the characters that appear in the word and are skipped should be excluded.

## Final Algorithm

```python
def solve():
    t = int(input())
    
    for _ in range(t):
        word = input().strip()
        n = int(input())
        skipped_letters = input().split()
        
        # Convert skipped letters to set for O(1) lookup
        skipped_set = set(skipped_letters)
        
        # Calculate total weight of the word
        total_weight = 0
        
        # For each character in the word
        for char in word:
            # If character is not skipped, add its weight (a=1, b=2, etc.)
            if char not in skipped_set:
                weight = ord(char) - ord('a') + 1
                total_weight += weight
        
        print(total_weight)

solve()
```

- b: skipped → contributes 0

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We need to calculate the weight of a word where each letter has a weight:
   - a = 1, b = 2, c = 3, ..., z = 26

2. Prof. M skips some letters, and those skipped letters contribute 0 to the weight

3. The weight of the word is the sum of weights of all characters in the word, minus the weights of skipped characters that appear in the word

4. For each test case:
   - We get a word W
   - We get N (number of skipped letters)
   - We get N letters that are skipped

## Step-by-step Solution

### Step 1: Understand the weight calculation
- Normal weight of a letter: position in alphabet (a=1, b=2, etc.)
- If a letter is skipped, its contribution is 0
- We need to sum the weights of all characters in the word, but subtract the weights of skipped characters that appear

### Step 2: Algorithm
1. For each test case:
   - Read the word W
   - Read N (number of skipped letters)
   - Read N skipped letters
   - Create a set of skipped letters for O(1) lookup
   - Calculate total weight by summing up the weight of each character in W
   - For each character in W, if it's in the skipped set, we don't add its weight
   - So we just sum the weights of characters that are NOT in the skipped set

### Step 3: Implementation details
- For a character, its weight = ord(char) - ord('a') + 1
- We need to check if each character in the word is skipped
- If skipped, skip adding its weight; otherwise add its weight

### Step 4: Trace through examples

**Example 1: "abcd", skip "a", "c"**
- a: weight 1, skipped → add 0
- b: weight 2, not skipped → add 2  
- c: weight 3, skipped → add 0
- d: weight 4, not skipped → add 4
- Total = 0 + 2 + 0 + 4 = 6 ✓

**Example 2: "aaaa", skip "a"**
- a: weight 1, skipped → add 0
- All 'a' characters are skipped → total = 0 ✓

**Example 3: "ababab", skip "a", "b"**
- All characters are skipped → total = 0 ✓

## Code Implementation

```python
t = int(input())

for _ in range(t):
    word = input().strip()
    n = int(input())
    skipped_letters = input().split()
    
    # Convert skipped letters to a set for O(1) lookup
    skipped_set = set(skipped_letters)
    
    # Calculate total weight
    total_weight = 0
    
    for char in word:
        # Calculate weight of current character
        char_weight = ord(char) - ord('a') + 1
        
        # If character is not skipped, add its weight
        if char not in skipped_set:
            total_weight += char_weight
    
    print(total_weight)
```

The solution works as follows:
1. Read number of test cases
2. For each test case:
   - Read the word
   - Read number of skipped letters and the skipped letters
   - Store skipped letters in a set for fast lookup
   - Iterate through each character in the word
   - Calculate character weight (position in alphabet)
   - If character is not in the skipped set, add its weight to total
   - Print the total weight

Time Complexity: O(|W|) per test case
Space Complexity: O(N) for storing skipped letters

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We need to calculate the weight of a word where each character has a weight:
   - 'a' = 1, 'b' = 2, ..., 'z' = 26
2. Prof. M skips some letters, and those skipped letters contribute 0 to the weight
3. We need to find the total weight of the word after accounting for skipped letters

## Step-by-Step Solution

### Step 1: Analyze the problem
- For each character in the word, if it's skipped, its weight is 0
- If it's not skipped, its weight is its position in alphabet (a=1, b=2, etc.)
- Total weight = sum of weights of non-skipped characters

### Step 2: Algorithm
1. Read number of test cases T
2. For each test case:
   - Read the word W
   - Read N (number of skipped letters)
   - Read N skipped letters
   - Create a set of skipped letters for O(1) lookup
   - Calculate total weight by iterating through each character in W
   - If character is not in skipped set, add its weight (ord(char) - ord('a') + 1)
   - Print the total weight

### Step 3: Trace through examples

**Example 1: "abcd", skip "a,c"**
- a: skipped → 0
- b: not skipped → 2
- c: skipped → 0
- d: not skipped → 4
- Total = 0 + 2 + 0 + 4 = 6 ✓

**Example 2: "aaaa", skip "a"**
- a: skipped → 0 (all four a's)
- Total = 0 ✓

**Example 3: "ababab", skip "a,b"**
- All characters are skipped → total = 0 ✓

### Step 4: Implementation

```python
t = int(input())

for _ in range(t):
    word = input().strip()
    n = int(input())
    skipped_letters = input().strip().split()
    
    # Convert skipped letters to set for O(1) lookup
    skipped_set = set(skipped_letters)
    
    # Calculate total weight
    total_weight = 0
    for char in word:
        if char not in skipped_set:
            # Weight of character = position in alphabet (a=1, b=2, etc.)
            weight = ord(char) - ord('a') + 1
            total_weight += weight
    
    print(total_weight)
```

The solution works as follows:
1. Read T test cases
2. For each test case, read the word, number of skipped letters, and the skipped letters
3. Store skipped letters in a set for efficient lookup
4. Iterate through each character in the word
5. If the character is not in the skipped set, add its alphabet position (ord(char) - ord('a') + 1) to the total weight
6. Print the total weight

Time complexity: O(|W|) per test case
Space complexity: O(N) for storing skipped letters

Problem

View Problem Statement 
You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

Recently Vladik discovered a new entertainment — coding bots for social networks. He would like to use machine learning in his bots so now he want to prepare some learning data for them.

At first, he need to download t chats. Vladik coded a script which should have downloaded the chats, however, something went wrong. In particular, some of the messages have no information of their sender. It is known that if a person sends several messages in a row, they all are merged into a single message. It means that there could not be two or more messages in a row with the same sender. Moreover, a sender never mention himself in his messages.

Vladik wants to recover senders of all the messages so that each two neighboring messages will have different senders and no sender will mention himself in his messages.

He has no idea of how to do this, and asks you for help. Help Vladik to recover senders in each of the chats!


-----Input-----

The first line contains single integer t (1 ≤ t ≤ 10) — the number of chats. The t chats follow. Each chat is given in the following format.

The first line of each chat description contains single integer n (1 ≤ n ≤ 100) — the number of users in the chat.

The next line contains n space-separated distinct usernames. Each username consists of lowercase and uppercase English letters and digits. The usernames can't start with a digit. Two usernames are different even if they differ only with letters' case. The length of username is positive and doesn't exceed 10 characters.

The next line contains single integer m (1 ≤ m ≤ 100) — the number of messages in the chat. The next m line contain the messages in the following formats, one per line:   <username>:<text> — the format of a message with known sender. The username should appear in the list of usernames of the chat.  <?>:<text> — the format of a message with unknown sender. 

The text of a message can consist of lowercase and uppercase English letter, digits, characters '.' (dot), ',' (comma), '!' (exclamation mark), '?' (question mark) and ' ' (space). The text doesn't contain trailing spaces. The length of the text is positive and doesn't exceed 100 characters.

We say that a text mention a user if his username appears in the text as a word. In other words, the username appears in a such a position that the two characters before and after its appearance either do not exist or are not English letters or digits. For example, the text "Vasya, masha13 and Kate!" can mention users "Vasya", "masha13", "and" and "Kate", but not "masha".

It is guaranteed that in each chat no known sender mention himself in his messages and there are no two neighboring messages with the same known sender.


-----Output-----

Print the information about the t chats in the following format:

If it is not possible to recover senders, print single line "Impossible" for this chat. Otherwise print m messages in the following format:

<username>:<text>

If there are multiple answers, print any of them.


-----Examples-----
Input
1
2
Vladik netman
2
?: Hello, Vladik!
?: Hi

Output
netman: Hello, Vladik!
Vladik: Hi

Input
1
2
netman vladik
3
netman:how are you?
?:wrong message
vladik:im fine

Output
Impossible

Input
2
3
netman vladik Fedosik
2
?: users are netman, vladik, Fedosik
vladik: something wrong with this chat
4
netman tigerrrrr banany2001 klinchuh
4
?: tigerrrrr, banany2001, klinchuh, my favourite team ever, are you ready?
klinchuh: yes, coach!
?: yes, netman
banany2001: yes of course.

Output
Impossible
netman: tigerrrrr, banany2001, klinchuh, my favourite team ever, are you ready?
klinchuh: yes, coach!
tigerrrrr: yes, netman

### Answer: (use the provided format with backticks)

Model Response

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have t chats, each with n users and m messages
2. Some messages have known senders, some have unknown senders (marked as `?:`)
3. Constraints:
   - No two neighboring messages can have the same sender
   - A sender cannot mention themselves in their message
   - If a person sends multiple messages in a row, they're merged into one message (so no consecutive same senders)

## Key Observations

1. For messages with known senders, we need to ensure they don't mention themselves
2. For messages with unknown senders, we need to assign them senders such that:
   - The sender is different from their neighbors
   - The sender doesn't mention themselves in the message

## Algorithm Steps

1. Parse the input to get users, messages, and known sender constraints
2. For each message with unknown sender (`?:`), determine possible senders
3. Use backtracking or constraint satisfaction to assign senders
4. Check all constraints during assignment

## Step-by-Step Solution

### Step 1: Parse messages and identify constraints
- Extract known senders and their messages
- For each unknown message, find which users can send it (not mentioning themselves, different from adjacent senders)

### Step 2: Handle constraints
- For each unknown message, check if any sender would mention themselves
- Check if the sender is different from neighboring messages

### Step 3: Solve with constraint propagation
- Try to assign senders one by one
- Use backtracking if needed

### Step 4: Implementation

```python
def get_mentions(text, users):
    """Extract all usernames mentioned in text"""
    mentions = set()
    words = []
    i = 0
    while i < len(text):
        if text[i].isalnum():
            start = i
            while i < len(text) and text[i].isalnum():
                i += 1
            word = text[start:i]
            words.append(word)
        else:
            i += 1
    
    for word in words:
        if word in users:
            mentions.add(word)
    
    return mentions

def can_send(message_text, sender, users):
    """Check if sender can send this message (doesn't mention themselves)"""
    mentions = get_mentions(message_text, users)
    return sender not in mentions

def solve_chat(n, users, messages):
    """Solve the chat by assigning senders to unknown messages"""
    # Extract known messages and create a list with senders
    known_messages = []
    for i, msg in enumerate(messages):
        if msg.startswith('?'):
            known_messages.append((i, None))  # Unknown sender
        else:
            sender, text = msg.split(':', 1)
            known_messages.append((i, sender))
    
    # Get actual messages
    actual_messages = []
    for i, (msg_idx, sender) in enumerate(known_messages):
        if sender is None:
            actual_messages.append((msg_idx, None))
        else:
            actual_messages.append((msg_idx, sender))
    
    # Create a list of messages with senders
    message_senders = [None] * len(messages)
    
    # First, fill in known senders
    for i, (msg_idx, sender) in enumerate(known_messages):
        message_senders[msg_idx] = sender
    
    # Check if known senders mention themselves
    for i, sender in enumerate(message_senders):
        if sender is not None:
            text = messages[i]
            text_part = text.split(':', 1)[1]
            if sender in get_mentions(text_part, users):
                return "Impossible"
    
    # Now try to assign senders to unknown messages
    # We'll use a greedy approach: for each unknown message, pick a valid sender
    
    # First, check if the current assignment satisfies all constraints
    for i in range(len(messages) - 1):
        if message_senders[i] == message_senders[i + 1]:
            return "Impossible"
    
    # For each unknown message, find valid senders
    for i in range(len(messages)):
        if message_senders[i] is None:
            valid_senders = []
            for user in users:
                # Check if user can send this message (doesn't mention themselves)
                if can_send(messages[i].split(':', 1)[1], user, users):
                    # Check if it's different from neighbors
                    left_valid = True
                    right_valid = True
                    
                    # Check left neighbor (if exists)
                    if i > 0:
                        left_valid = message_senders[i-1] != user
                    # Check right neighbor (if exists)
                    if i < len(messages) - 1:
                        right_valid = message_senders[i+1] != user
                    
                    if left_valid and right_valid:
                        valid_senders.append(user)
            
            # If no valid sender found, impossible
            if not valid_senders:
                return "Impossible"
            
            # Pick any valid sender
            message_senders[i] = valid_senders[0]
    
    # Final check: no consecutive same senders
    for i in range(len(messages) - 1):
        if message_senders[i] == message_senders[i + 1]:
            return "Impossible"
    
    # Check all senders don't mention themselves
    for i, sender in enumerate(message_senders):
        if sender is not None:
            text = messages[i].split(':', 1)[1]
            if sender in get_mentions(text, users):
                return "Impossible"
    
    # Build result
    result = []
    for i, sender in enumerate(message_senders):
        if sender is None:
            result.append(f"?:{messages[i].split(':', 1)[1]}")
        else:
            result.append(f"{sender}:{messages[i].split(':', 1)[1]}")
    
    return result

# Main solution
t = int(input())
for _ in range(t):
    n = int(input())
    users = input().split()
    m = int(input())
    messages = []
    for _ in range(m):
        messages.append(input())
    
    result = solve_chat(n, users, messages)
    
    if result == "Impossible":
        print("Impossible")
    else:
        for msg in result:
            print(msg)
```

Wait, I see an issue with my approach. Let me reconsider the problem. The issue is that when I assign senders, I need to consider the messages are given in order, and I have to ensure no two consecutive messages have the same sender.

Let me fix the implementation:

```python
def get_mentions(text, users):
    """Extract all usernames mentioned in text"""
    mentions = set()
    # Split text into words (alphanumeric sequences)
    words = []
    i = 0
    while i < len(text):
        if text[i].isalnum():
            start = i
            while i < len(text) and text[i].isalnum():
                i += 1
            word = text[start:i]
            if word in users:  # Only add if it's a valid user
                mentions.add(word)
        else:
            i += 1
    return mentions

def solve_chat(n, users, messages):
    """Solve the chat by assigning senders to unknown messages"""
    # Create a list to store the final sender for each message
    senders = [None] * len(messages)
    
    # First, assign known senders
    for i, msg in enumerate(messages):
        if msg.startswith('?'):
            senders[i] = None
        else:
            sender, text = msg.split(':', 1)
            senders[i] = sender
    
    # Check if known senders mention themselves
    for i, sender in enumerate(senders):
        if sender is not None:
            text = messages[i].split(':', 1)[1]
            if sender in get_mentions(text, users):
                return "Impossible"
    
    # Check for consecutive same senders in known messages
    for i in range(len(messages) - 1):
        if senders[i] is not None and senders[i] == senders[i + 1]:
            return "Impossible"
    
    # For each unknown message, find a valid sender
    for i in range(len(messages)):
        if senders[i] is None:
            valid_senders = []
            
            for user in users:
                # Check if user can send this message (doesn't mention themselves)
                if user in get_mentions(messages[i].split(':', 1)[1], users):
                    continue  # Can't send if mentioning themselves
                
                # Check if it's different from left neighbor
                left_ok = True
                if i > 0:
                    left_ok = senders[i-1] != user
                
                # Check if it's different from right neighbor
                right_ok = True
                if i < len(messages) - 1:
                    right_ok = senders[i+1] != user
                
                if left_ok and right_ok:
                    valid_senders.append(user)
            
            # If no valid sender found, impossible
            if not valid_senders:
                return "Impossible"
            
            # Assign first valid sender
            senders[i] = valid_senders[0]
    
    # Final check: no consecutive same senders
    for i in range(len(messages) - 1):
        if senders[i] == senders[i + 1]:
            return "Impossible"
    
    # Check all senders don't mention themselves
    for i, sender in enumerate(senders):
        if sender is not None:
            text = messages[i].split(':', 1)[1]
            if sender in get_mentions(text, users):
                return "Impossible"
    
    # Build result
    result = []
    for i, sender in enumerate(senders):
        if sender is None:
            result.append(f"?:{messages[i].split(':', 1)[1]}")
        else:
            result.append(f"{sender}:{messages[i].split(':', 1)[1]}")
    
    return result

# Main solution
t = int(input())
for _ in range(t):
    n = int(input())
    users = input().split()
    m = int(input())
    messages = []
    for _ in range(m):
        messages.append(input())
    
    result = solve_chat(n, users, messages)
    
    if result == "Impossible":
        print("Impossible")
    else:
        for msg in result:
            print(msg)
```

def get_mentions(text, users):
    """Extract all usernames mentioned in text"""
    mentions = set()
    # Split text into words (alphanumeric sequences)
    words = []
    i = 0
    while i < len(text):
        if text[i].isalnum():
            start = i
            while i < len(text) and text[i].isalnum():
                i += 1
            word = text[start:i]
            if word in users:  # Only add if it's a valid user
                mentions.add(word)
        else:
            i += 1
    return mentions

def solve_chat(n, users, messages):
    """Solve the chat by assigning senders to unknown messages"""
    # Create a list to store the final sender for each message
    senders = [None] * len(messages)
    
    # First, assign known senders
    for i, msg in enumerate(messages):
        if msg.startswith('?'):
            senders[i] = None
        else:
            sender, text = msg.split(':', 1)
            senders[i] = sender
    
    # Check if known senders mention themselves
    for i, sender in enumerate(senders):
        if sender is not None:
            text = messages[i].split(':', 1)[1]
            if sender in get_mentions(text, users):
                return "Impossible"
    
    # Check for consecutive same senders in known messages
    for i in range(len(messages) - 1):
        if senders[i] is not None and senders[i] == senders[i + 1]:
            return "Impossible"
    
    # For each unknown message, find a valid sender
    for i in range(len(messages)):
        if senders[i] is None:
            valid_senders = []
            
            for user in users:
                # Check if user can send this message (doesn't mention themselves)
                if user in get_mentions(messages[i].split(':', 1)[1], users):
                    continue  # Can't send if mentioning themselves
                
                # Check if it's different from left neighbor
                left_ok = True
                if i > 0:
                    left_ok = senders[i-1] != user
                
                # Check if it's different from right neighbor
                right_ok = True
                if i < len(messages) - 1:
                    right_ok = senders[i+1] != user
                
                if left_ok and right_ok:
                    valid_senders.append(user)
            
            # If no valid sender found, impossible
            if not valid_senders:
                return "Impossible"
            
            # Assign first valid sender
            senders[i] = valid_senders[0]
    
    # Final check: no consecutive same senders
    for i in range(len(messages) - 1):
        if senders[i] == senders[i + 1]:
            return "Impossible"
    
    # Check all senders don't mention themselves
    for i, sender in enumerate(senders):
        if sender is not None:
            text = messages[i].split(':', 1)[1]
            if sender in get_mentions(text, users):
                return "Impossible"

Problem

View Problem Statement 
You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

Recently Vladik discovered a new entertainment — coding bots for social networks. He would like to use machine learning in his bots so now he want to prepare some learning data for them.

At first, he need to download t chats. Vladik coded a script which should have downloaded the chats, however, something went wrong. In particular, some of the messages have no information of their sender. It is known that if a person sends several messages in a row, they all are merged into a single message. It means that there could not be two or more messages in a row with the same sender. Moreover, a sender never mention himself in his messages.

Vladik wants to recover senders of all the messages so that each two neighboring messages will have different senders and no sender will mention himself in his messages.

He has no idea of how to do this, and asks you for help. Help Vladik to recover senders in each of the chats!


-----Input-----

The first line contains single integer t (1 ≤ t ≤ 10) — the number of chats. The t chats follow. Each chat is given in the following format.

The first line of each chat description contains single integer n (1 ≤ n ≤ 100) — the number of users in the chat.

The next line contains n space-separated distinct usernames. Each username consists of lowercase and uppercase English letters and digits. The usernames can't start with a digit. Two usernames are different even if they differ only with letters' case. The length of username is positive and doesn't exceed 10 characters.

The next line contains single integer m (1 ≤ m ≤ 100) — the number of messages in the chat. The next m line contain the messages in the following formats, one per line:   <username>:<text> — the format of a message with known sender. The username should appear in the list of usernames of the chat.  <?>:<text> — the format of a message with unknown sender. 

The text of a message can consist of lowercase and uppercase English letter, digits, characters '.' (dot), ',' (comma), '!' (exclamation mark), '?' (question mark) and ' ' (space). The text doesn't contain trailing spaces. The length of the text is positive and doesn't exceed 100 characters.

We say that a text mention a user if his username appears in the text as a word. In other words, the username appears in a such a position that the two characters before and after its appearance either do not exist or are not English letters or digits. For example, the text "Vasya, masha13 and Kate!" can mention users "Vasya", "masha13", "and" and "Kate", but not "masha".

It is guaranteed that in each chat no known sender mention himself in his messages and there are no two neighboring messages with the same known sender.


-----Output-----

Print the information about the t chats in the following format:

If it is not possible to recover senders, print single line "Impossible" for this chat. Otherwise print m messages in the following format:

<username>:<text>

If there are multiple answers, print any of them.


-----Examples-----
Input
1
2
Vladik netman
2
?: Hello, Vladik!
?: Hi

Output
netman: Hello, Vladik!
Vladik: Hi

Input
1
2
netman vladik
3
netman:how are you?
?:wrong message
vladik:im fine

Output
Impossible

Input
2
3
netman vladik Fedosik
2
?: users are netman, vladik, Fedosik
vladik: something wrong with this chat
4
netman tigerrrrr banany2001 klinchuh
4
?: tigerrrrr, banany2001, klinchuh, my favourite team ever, are you ready?
klinchuh: yes, coach!
?: yes, netman
banany2001: yes of course.

Output
Impossible

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have t chats, each with n users and m messages
2. Some messages have known senders, some have unknown senders (marked as "?")
3. Constraints:
   - No two consecutive messages can have the same sender
   - A sender cannot mention themselves in their message
   - If a person sends multiple messages in a row, they're merged into one message

## Key Observations

1. For unknown senders (marked as "?"), we need to assign senders that:
   - Are different from the previous message's sender
   - Are different from the next message's sender (if it exists)
   - Don't mention themselves in their message text

2. For a message to not mention itself, we need to check if the username appears as a word in the text (with proper word boundaries).

3. We need to handle the constraints in sequence:
   - First, check if the known senders violate any constraints (they shouldn't mention themselves)
   - Then, assign senders to unknown messages while respecting the constraints

## Algorithm

1. Parse input and extract known messages
2. For each unknown message, determine valid senders based on:
   - Not equal to previous message sender
   - Not equal to next message sender (if exists)
   - Does not appear in the message text as a word
3. Use a greedy approach: assign senders to unknown messages one by one
4. If at any point no valid sender exists, return "Impossible"

## Step-by-step Solution

```python
def extract_words(text):
    """Extract words from text (sequences of letters and digits)"""
    words = []
    i = 0
    while i < len(text):
        if text[i].isalnum():
            j = i
            while j < len(text) and text[j].isalnum():
                j += 1
            words.append(text[i:j])
            i = j
        else:
            i += 1
    return words

def word_in_text(text, word):
    """Check if word appears in text as a word"""
    words = extract_words(text)
    return word in words

def solve_chat():
    n = int(input())
    usernames = input().split()
    m = int(input())
    
    messages = []
    for _ in range(m):
        line = input().strip()
        parts = line.split(':', 1)
        if parts[0] == '?':
            messages.append(('unknown', parts[1]))
        else:
            messages.append((parts[0], parts[1]))
    
    # First, verify known senders don't mention themselves
    for i in range(m):
        if messages[i][0] != 'unknown':
            sender = messages[i][0]
            text = messages[i][1]
            if word_in_text(text, sender):
                return "Impossible"
    
    # Check if consecutive messages have same sender (but this is already guaranteed)
    # Now assign senders to unknown messages
    
    # Create a list to store final senders
    result = []
    
    # First pass: assign senders greedily
    for i in range(m):
        if messages[i][0] == 'unknown':
            valid_senders = []
            
            # Can't be same as previous sender
            prev_sender = result[i-1] if i > 0 else None
            
            # Can't be same as next sender (if exists)
            next_sender = None
            if i < m - 1:
                if messages[i+1][0] != 'unknown':
                    next_sender = messages[i+1][0]
                else:
                    # For next unknown, we'll determine it later
                    pass
            
            # Check all possible senders
            for username in usernames:
                # Check if this username is valid
                if prev_sender is not None and username == prev_sender:
                    continue
                
                # Check if this username appears in the message text
                if word_in_text(messages[i][1], username):
                    continue
                
                # For next sender check, we need to make sure it doesn't conflict
                # But we can't guarantee next one will work, so we just check the immediate constraints
                
                # Check if we need to avoid the next known sender
                if next_sender is not None and username == next_sender:
                    continue
                    
                valid_senders.append(username)
            
            # If no valid sender found, impossible
            if not valid_senders:
                return "Impossible"
            
            # Choose any valid sender (greedy choice)
            result.append(valid_senders[0])
        else:
            result.append(messages[i][0])
    
    # After assignment, verify constraints
    # Check consecutive messages have different senders
    for i in range(m-1):
        if result[i] == result[i+1]:
            return "Impossible"
    
    # Verify that no sender mentions themselves
    for i in range(m):
        sender = result[i]
        text = messages[i][1]
        if word_in_text(text, sender):
            return "Impossible"
    
    # Build output
    output_lines = []
    for i in range(m):
        if result[i] == 'unknown':
            # This should not happen
            pass
        output_lines.append(f"{result[i]}:{messages[i][1]}")
    
    return '\n'.join(output_lines)

# Main execution
t = int(input())
for _ in range(t):
    result = solve_chat()
    if result == "Impossible":
        print("Impossible")
    else:
        print(result)
```

```python
def extract_words(text):
    """Extract words from text (sequences of letters and digits)"""
    words = []

    return word in words
```

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have t chats, each with n users and m messages
2. Some messages have known senders (format: `username:text`), others have unknown senders (format: `?:text`)
3. Constraints:
   - No two neighboring messages can have the same sender
   - A sender cannot mention themselves in their message
   - If a person sends multiple messages in a row, they're merged into one message (so no consecutive same senders)

## Key Insights

1. For unknown sender messages, we need to assign a username such that:
   - It's different from the previous message's sender
   - It's different from the next message's sender (if it exists)
   - The assigned username doesn't appear in the text of the message

2. We can use a backtracking or greedy approach. Since constraints are small (n, m ≤ 100), we can try to assign senders one by one.

3. For each unknown message, we need to check which usernames are valid:
   - Not the previous message's sender
   - Not the next message's sender (if next exists)
   - Not mentioned in the message text

## Step-by-Step Solution

1. Parse input to get users, messages
2. For each message with unknown sender (starting with `?:`), determine valid senders
3. Use a greedy assignment: for each unknown message, pick any valid sender from the available options
4. Check if the assignment is valid (no consecutive same senders, no self-mention)

Let me trace through the examples:

Example 1:
- Users: Vladik, netman
- Messages: `?: Hello, Vladik!`, `?: Hi`
- First message: can't be Vladik (mentions Vladik), so must be netman
- Second message: can't be netman (previously), so must be Vladik
- Valid assignment: netman: Hello, Vladik!, Vladik: Hi

Example 2:
- Users: netman, vladik
- Messages: netman:how are you?, ?:wrong message, vladik:im fine
- First message: netman sends "how are you?" - valid
- Third message: vladik sends "im fine" - valid  
- Middle message: unknown, can't be netman (previous), can't be vladik (next), so impossible

## Implementation

```python
def extract_mentions(text, usernames):
    """Extract all mentions from text (words that match usernames)"""
    mentions = set()
    words = []
    i = 0
    while i < len(text):
        if text[i].isalnum():
            start = i
            while i < len(text) and text[i].isalnum():
                i += 1
            word = text[start:i]
            words.append(word)
        else:
            i += 1
    
    # Check which words in words match usernames
    for word in words:
        if word in usernames:
            mentions.add(word)
    
    return mentions

def solve_chat(users, messages):
    n = len(messages)
    if n == 0:
        return None
    
    # Create list of message tuples (sender, text)
    result = []
    for i, msg in enumerate(messages):
        if msg.startswith('?'):
            result.append(None)  # unknown sender
            text = msg[2:].strip()
        else:
            # Split by colon
            parts = msg.split(':', 1)
            result.append(parts[0])
            text = parts[1]
    
    # We need to assign senders to unknown messages
    # Use greedy approach: for each unknown message, try all valid options
    
    # Try to assign senders
    for i in range(n):
        if result[i] is not None:
            continue
            
        # Find valid senders for message i
        valid_senders = []
        for user in users:
            # Skip if user mentions themselves in the message
            mentions = extract_mentions(messages[i][2:], users)
            if user in mentions:
                continue
                
            # Skip if user is same as previous message (if exists)
            if i > 0 and result[i-1] == user:
                continue
                
            # Skip if user is same as next message (if exists)
            if i < n-1 and result[i+1] == user:
                continue
                
            valid_senders.append(user)
        
        if not valid_senders:
            return "Impossible"
        
        # Pick any valid sender (greedy choice)
        result[i] = valid_senders[0]
    
    # Final validation: check if any consecutive messages have same sender
    for i in range(n-1):
        if result[i] == result[i+1]:
            return "Impossible"
    
    # Reconstruct final messages
    final_messages = []
    for i, msg in enumerate(messages):
        if msg.startswith('?'):
            final_messages.append(f"{result[i]}:{msg[2:].strip()}")
        else:
            parts = msg.split(':', 1)
            final_messages.append(f"{parts[0]}:{parts[1]}")
    
    return final_messages

def main():
    t = int(input())
    
    for _ in range(t):
        n = int(input())
        users = input().split()
        m = int(input())
        messages = []
        for _ in range(m):
            messages.append(input())
        
        result = solve_chat(users, messages)
        
        if result == "Impossible":
            print("Impossible")
        else:
            for msg in result:
                print(msg)

main()
```

Problem

View Problem Statement 
You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

Recently Vladik discovered a new entertainment — coding bots for social networks. He would like to use machine learning in his bots so now he want to prepare some learning data for them.

At first, he need to download t chats. Vladik coded a script which should have downloaded the chats, however, something went wrong. In particular, some of the messages have no information of their sender. It is known that if a person sends several messages in a row, they all are merged into a single message. It means that there could not be two or more messages in a row with the same sender. Moreover, a sender never mention himself in his messages.

Vladik wants to recover senders of all the messages so that each two neighboring messages will have different senders and no sender will mention himself in his messages.

He has no idea of how to do this, and asks you for help. Help Vladik to recover senders in each of the chats!


-----Input-----

The first line contains single integer t (1 ≤ t ≤ 10) — the number of chats. The t chats follow. Each chat is given in the following format.

The first line of each chat description contains single integer n (1 ≤ n ≤ 100) — the number of users in the chat.

The next line contains n space-separated distinct usernames. Each username consists of lowercase and uppercase English letters and digits. The usernames can't start with a digit. Two usernames are different even if they differ only with letters' case. The length of username is positive and doesn't exceed 10 characters.

The next line contains single integer m (1 ≤ m ≤ 100) — the number of messages in the chat. The next m line contain the messages in the following formats, one per line:   <username>:<text> — the format of a message with known sender. The username should appear in the list of usernames of the chat.  <?>:<text> — the format of a message with unknown sender. 

The text of a message can consist of lowercase and uppercase English letter, digits, characters '.' (dot), ',' (comma), '!' (exclamation mark), '?' (question mark) and ' ' (space). The text doesn't contain trailing spaces. The length of the text is positive and doesn't exceed 100 characters.

We say that a text mention a user if his username appears in the text as a word. In other words, the username appears in a such a position that the two characters before and after its appearance either do not exist or are not English letters or digits. For example, the text "Vasya, masha13 and Kate!" can mention users "Vasya", "masha13", "and" and "Kate", but not "masha".

It is guaranteed that in each chat no known sender mention himself in his messages and there are no two neighboring messages with the same known sender.


-----Output-----

Print the information about the t chats in the following format:

If it is not possible to recover senders, print single line "Impossible" for this chat. Otherwise print m messages in the following format:

<username>:<text>

If there are multiple answers, print any of them.


-----Examples-----
Input
1
2
Vladik netman
2
?: Hello, Vladik!
?: Hi

Output
netman: Hello, Vladik!
Vladik: Hi

Input
1
2
netman vladik
3
netman:how are you?
?:wrong message
vladik:im fine

Output
Impossible

Input
2
3
netman vladik Fedosik
2
?: users are netman, vladik, Fedosik
vladik: something wrong with this chat
4
netman tigerrrrr banany2001 klinchuh
4
?: tigerrrrr, banany2001, klinchuh, my favourite team ever, are you ready?
klinchuh: yes, coach!
?: yes, netman
banany2001: yes of course.

Output
Impossible
netman: tigerrrrr, banany2001, klinchuh, my favourite team ever, are you ready?
klinchuh: yes, coach!
tigerrrrr: yes, netman

### Answer: (use the provided format with backticks)

Model Response

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have t chats, each with n users and m messages
2. Some messages have known senders (format: `<username>:<text>`)
3. Some messages have unknown senders (format: `?:<text>`)
4. Constraints:
   - No two neighboring messages can have the same sender
   - A sender cannot mention themselves in their message
   - If someone sends multiple messages in a row, they're merged into one message

## Key Insights

1. For unknown sender messages, we need to assign a sender such that:
   - It's different from the previous message's sender
   - It's different from the next message's sender (if exists)
   - The sender doesn't mention themselves in the message text

2. We can use a greedy approach:
   - Process messages from left to right
   - For each unknown message, try different users as senders
   - Ensure constraints are met

3. For impossibility:
   - If a user is mentioned in a message, they cannot be the sender
   - If no valid sender exists for an unknown message, it's impossible

## Step-by-Step Solution

### Step 1: Parse input and extract information
- Read number of chats
- For each chat: read users, messages

### Step 2: Function to check if a username appears as a word in text
- Check if the username appears as a word (bounded by non-alphanumeric characters)

### Step 3: Greedy assignment with backtracking
- Process messages from left to right
- For each unknown message, try each available user
- Check constraints with previous and next messages

### Step 4: Handle the constraints
- Different from previous sender
- Different from next sender (when processing)
- Doesn't mention itself

```python
def is_mentioned(text, username):
    """Check if username appears as a word in text"""
    # Split text into words (using non-alphanumeric as boundaries)
    words = []
    current_word = ""
    for char in text:
        if char.isalnum():
            current_word += char
        else:
            if current_word:
                words.append(current_word)
                current_word = ""
    if current_word:
        words.append(current_word)
    
    # Check if username appears as a word
    username_clean = username.lower()
    for word in words:
        word_clean = word.lower()
        if word_clean == username_clean:
            return True
    return False

def solve_chat(users, messages):
    n = len(users)
    m = len(messages)
    
    # Convert users to list for easier manipulation
    user_list = [user.lower() for user in users]
    
    # Create a copy of messages to modify
    result = [None] * m
    current_sender = None
    
    # First, fix messages with known senders
    for i, msg in enumerate(messages):
        if msg.startswith('?'):
            # Unknown sender, will be assigned later
            result[i] = None
        else:
            # Known sender
            sender_part = msg.split(':', 1)[0]
            result[i] = (sender_part, msg.split(':', 1)[1])
            current_sender = sender_part
    
    # Process known senders to build constraints
    fixed_senders = []
    for i, msg in enumerate(messages):
        if msg.startswith('?'):
            pass
        else:
            fixed_senders.append((i, msg.split(':', 1)[0]))
    
    # Try to assign senders for unknown messages
    # We'll use a greedy approach from left to right
    for i in range(m):
        if result[i] is not None:
            # Already fixed
            current_sender = result[i][0]
        else:
            # Unknown sender, find a valid one
            found = False
            
            # Try each user
            for user in user_list:
                # Skip if user mentions themselves
                if is_mentioned(messages[i], user):
                    continue
                
                # Skip if it has same sender as previous message
                if i > 0 and result[i-1][0] == user:
                    continue
                
                # Skip if it will have same sender as next message (if exists)
                if i < m - 1:
                    next_sender = None
                    if result[i+1] is not None:
                        next_sender = result[i+1][0]
                    if next_sender == user:
                        continue
                
                # If we reach here, this user works
                result[i] = (user, messages[i])
                found = True
                break
            
            if not found:
                return "Impossible"
    
    # Final check: verify no two neighbors have same sender
    for i in range(m - 1):
        if result[i][0] == result[i+1][0]:
            return "Impossible"
    
    return result

def main():
    t = int(input())
    
    for _ in range(t):
        n = int(input())
        users = input().split()
        m = int(input())
        messages = []
        for _ in range(m):
            messages.append(input().strip())
        
        # Solve this chat
        result = solve_chat(users, messages)
        
        if result == "Impossible":
            print("Impossible")
        else:
            for msg in result:
                if msg is not None:
                    print(f"{msg[0]}:{msg[1]}")

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

```python
def is_mentioned(text, username):
    """Check if username appears as a word in text"""
    # Split text into words (using non-alphanumeric as boundaries)
    words = []
    current_word = ""
    for char in text:
        if char.isalnum():
            current_word += char
        else:
            if current_word:
                words.append(current_word)
                current_word = ""
    if current_word:
        words.append(current_word)
    
    # Check if username appears as a word (case-insensitive)
    username_clean = username.lower()
    for word in words:
        word_clean = word.lower()
        if word_clean == username_clean:
            return True
    return False

def solve_chat(users, messages):
    n = len(users)
    m = len(messages)
    
    # Convert to lowercase for comparison
    user_list = [user.lower() for user in users]
    
    # Create result array
    result = [None] * m
    
    # Process each message
    for i in range(m):
        if messages[i].startswith('?'):
            # Unknown sender - find valid sender
            valid_senders = []
            for user in user_list:
                # Skip if user mentions themselves
                if is_mentioned(messages[i], user):
                    continue
                
                # Skip if same as previous message
                if i > 0 and result[i-1] is not None and result[i-1][0] == user:
                    continue
                
                # Skip if same as next message (we'll check later)
                # We can check next message when processing it
                valid_senders.append(user)
            
            if not valid_senders:
                return "Impossible"
            
            # Choose first valid sender (any valid choice)
            result[i] = (valid_senders[0], messages[i])
        else:
            # Known sender
            sender, text = messages[i].split(':', 1)
            result[i] = (sender.lower(), text)
    
    # Final check: ensure no two neighbors have same sender
    for i in range(m - 1):
        if result[i][0] == result[i + 1][0]:
            return "Impossible"
    
    return result

def main():
    t = int(input())
    
    for _ in range(t):
        n = int(input())
        users = input().split()
        m = int(input())
        messages = []
        for _ in range(m):
            messages.append(input().strip())
        
        # Solve this chat
        result = solve_chat(users, messages)
        
        if result == "Impossible":
            print("Impossible")
        else:
            for msg in result:
                if msg is not None:
                    print(f"{msg[0]}:{msg[1]}")

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

            return "Impossible"

        else:

                if msg is not None:
                    print(f"{msg[0]}:{msg[1]}")

    main()

The next line contains n space-separated distinct usernames. Each username consists of lowercase and uppercase English letters and digits. The usernames can't start with a digit. Two usernames are different even if they differ only with letters' case. The length of username is positive and doesn't exceed 10 characters.

The next line contains single integer m (1 ≤ m ≤ 100) — the number of messages in the chat. The next m line contain the messages in the following formats, one per line:   <username>:<text> — the format of a message with known sender. The username should appear in the list of usernames of the chat.  <?>:<text> — the format of a message with unknown sender. 

The text of a message can consist of lowercase and uppercase English letter, digits, characters '.' (dot), ',' (comma), '!' (exclamation mark), '?' (question mark) and ' ' (space). The text doesn't contain trailing spaces. The length of the text is positive and doesn't exceed 100 characters.

We say that a text mention a user if his username appears in the text as a word. In other words, the username appears in a such a position that the two characters before and after its appearance either do not exist or are not English letters or digits. For example, the text "Vasya, masha13 and Kate!" can mention users "Vasya", "masha13", "and" and "Kate", but not "masha".

It is guaranteed that in each chat no known sender mention himself in his messages and there are no two neighboring messages with the same known sender.

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have t chats, each with n users and m messages
2. Some messages have known senders (format: `username:text`), others have unknown senders (format: `?:text`)
3. Constraints:
   - No two consecutive messages can have the same sender
   - A sender cannot mention themselves in their message (username must not appear as a word in the text)
4. We need to assign senders to all messages with unknown senders to satisfy these constraints

## Step-by-Step Solution

### Step 1: Parse Input and Understand Constraints

- For each chat, we have users, messages with known/unknown senders
- For unknown messages, we need to assign a sender from the available users
- The assignment must satisfy:
  - Neighboring messages have different senders
  - A sender doesn't mention themselves in their message

### Step 2: Algorithm Design

For each unknown message, we need to find a valid sender. The constraints are:
1. The sender can't be the same as the previous message's sender
2. The sender can't be mentioned in the message text (as a word)

We can use a greedy approach:
- Process messages from left to right
- For each unknown message, try all valid senders (excluding previous sender and those mentioned in text)
- If no valid sender exists, return "Impossible"

### Step 3: Word Mention Detection

To check if a username appears as a word in text:
- A word is a sequence of letters/digits bounded by non-alphanumeric characters or string boundaries
- We need to check if the username appears as a complete word

### Step 4: Implementation

```python
import re

def is_word_mentioned(text, username):
    """Check if username appears as a complete word in text"""
    # Find all words in text (sequences of alphanumeric characters)
    words = re.findall(r'[a-zA-Z0-9]+', text)
    return username in words

def solve_chat(users, messages):
    n = len(users)
    m = len(messages)
    
    # Convert users to a set for O(1) lookup
    user_set = set(users)
    
    # Store the sender of each message
    senders = [None] * m
    
    # Process each message
    for i in range(m):
        message = messages[i]
        if message.startswith('?:'):
            # Unknown sender
            text = message[2:].strip()
            
            # Previous sender (if exists)
            prev_sender = senders[i-1] if i > 0 else None
            
            # Find valid sender
            valid_senders = []
            for user in users:
                # Cannot be same as previous sender
                if prev_sender is not None and user == prev_sender:
                    continue
                
                # Cannot mention themselves in message
                if is_word_mentioned(text, user):
                    continue
                
                valid_senders.append(user)
            
            if not valid_senders:
                return None  # Impossible
            
            # Choose any valid sender (first one for simplicity)
            senders[i] = valid_senders[0]
        else:
            # Known sender
            parts = message.split(':')
            sender = parts[0]
            text = parts[1]
            senders[i] = sender
    
    # Verify that consecutive messages have different senders
    for i in range(1, m):
        if senders[i] == senders[i-1]:
            return None
    
    # Return the result
    result = []
    for i in range(m):
        if senders[i] is None:
            result.append(f"{senders[i]}:{messages[i][2:]}")
        else:
            result.append(f"{senders[i]}:{messages[i][2:]}")
    
    return result

def main():
    t = int(input())
    
    for _ in range(t):
        n = int(input())
        users = input().split()
        m = int(input())
        
        messages = []
        for _ in range(m):
            messages.append(input().strip())
        
        result = solve_chat(users, messages)
        
        if result is None:
            print("Impossible")
        else:
            for msg in result:
                print(msg)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

View Problem Statement 
You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

Recently Vladik discovered a new entertainment — coding bots for social networks. He would like to use machine learning in his bots so now he want to prepare some learning data for them.

At first, he need to download t chats. Vladik coded a script which should have downloaded the chats, however, something went wrong. It means that there could not be two or more messages in a row with the same sender. Moreover, a sender never mention himself in his messages.

Vladik wants to recover senders of all the messages so that each two neighboring messages will have different senders and no sender will mention himself in his messages.

He has no idea of how to do this, and asks you for help. Help Vladik to recover senders in each of the chats!

Model Response

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have t chats, each with n users and m messages
2. Some messages have known senders, some have unknown senders (marked as "?")
3. Constraints:
   - No two consecutive messages can have the same sender
   - A sender cannot mention themselves in their message (the username must not appear as a word in the text)
   - If a person sends multiple messages in a row, they merge into one message (so no consecutive same senders)

## Solution Approach

1. **Parse Input**: Read all chats, users, and messages
2. **Handle Known Senders**: First, check if known senders violate constraints (mention themselves or consecutive same senders)
3. **Handle Unknown Senders**: For messages with "?", try to assign senders that:
   - Are different from their adjacent messages (left and right)
   - Don't mention themselves in their message text
4. **Validation**: After assignment, verify all constraints are satisfied

## Step-by-Step Algorithm

1. For each chat:
   - Extract users and messages
   - Check if known senders violate rules
   - For unknown messages, try to assign senders using backtracking or greedy approach
   - Validate final assignment

2. **Key insights**:
   - For messages with "?" at position i, the sender must be different from message i-1 and i+1 (if they exist)
   - The sender must not appear as a word in the message text
   - We need to check if the words in the text match the username pattern (surrounded by non-alphanumeric characters)

3. **Word extraction function**: Check if username appears as a word in text (need non-alphanumeric boundaries)

## Implementation

```python
def is_word_mentioned(text, username):
    """Check if username appears as a word in text"""
    # Convert to lowercase for case-insensitive comparison
    text_lower = text.lower()
    username_lower = username.lower()
    
    # Find all word boundaries
    words = []
    i = 0
    while i < len(text_lower):
        # Skip non-alphanumeric characters
        while i < len(text_lower) and not text_lower[i].isalnum():
            i += 1
        if i >= len(text_lower):
            break
            
        # Find end of word
        j = i
        while j < len(text_lower) and text_lower[j].isalnum():
            j += 1
            
        word = text_lower[i:j]
        words.append(word)
        i = j
    
    # Check if username appears as a word
    return username_lower in words

def solve_chat(users, messages):
    # Convert users to lowercase for comparison
    users_lower = [u.lower() for u in users]
    
    # Create a copy of messages with positions
    message_copy = []
    for msg in messages:
        parts = msg.split(':', 1)
        sender = parts[0]
        text = parts[1]
        message_copy.append((sender, text))
    
    # Check known sender constraints
    for i in range(len(message_copy)):
        sender, text = message_copy[i]
        if sender == '?':
            continue
            
        # Check if sender mentions himself
        if is_word_mentioned(text, sender):
            return "Impossible"
        
        # If consecutive messages have same sender, return impossible
        if i > 0 and message_copy[i-1][0] == sender:
            return "Impossible"
    
    # Now try to assign senders to unknown messages
    # We'll use a simple greedy approach: for each ?, try to assign a sender
    # that is different from neighbors and doesn't mention himself
    
    result = message_copy[:]
    
    for i in range(len(result)):
        if result[i][0] == '?':
            # Find possible senders that satisfy constraints
            possible_senders = []
            
            for user in users:
                user_lower = user.lower()
                # Check if this user mentions himself (after case conversion)
                if is_word_mentioned(result[i][1], user):
                    continue
                
                # Check constraints with neighbors
                valid = True
                
                # Check left neighbor (if exists)
                if i > 0 and result[i-1][0] != '?' and result[i-1][0].lower() == user.lower():
                    valid = False
                
                # Check right neighbor (if exists)
                if i < len(result) - 1 and result[i+1][0] != '?' and result[i+1][0].lower() == user.lower():
                    valid = False
                
                if valid:
                    possible_senders.append(user)
            
            if not possible_senders:
                return "Impossible"
            
            # Choose any valid sender
            result[i] = (possible_senders[0], result[i][1])
    
    # Final validation
    for i in range(len(result)):
        sender, text = result[i]
        if sender == '?':
            continue
            
        # Check if sender mentions himself
        if is_word_mentioned(text, sender):
            return "Impossible"
            
        # Check consecutive messages
        if i > 0 and result[i-1][0] == sender:
            return "Impossible"
            
        if i < len(result) - 1 and result[i+1][0] == sender:
            return "Impossible"
    
    return result

def solve():
    t = int(input())
    
    for _ in range(t):
        n = int(input())
        users = input().split()
        
        m = int(input())
        messages = []
        for _ in range(m):
            messages.append(input().strip())
        
        result = solve_chat(users, messages)
        
        if result == "Impossible":
            print("Impossible")
        else:
            for msg in result:
                print(f"{msg[0]}:{msg[1]}")

solve()
```
