```python
def is_word_mentioned(text, username):
    """Check if username appears as a word in text"""
    text_lower = text.lower()
    username_lower = username.lower()
    
    # Find all word boundaries
    i = 0
    while i < len(text_lower):
        # Skip non-alphanumeric characters
        while i < len(text_lower) and not text_lower[i].isalnum():
            i += 1
        if i >= len(text_lower):
            break
            
        # Find end of word
        j = i
        while j < len(text_lower) and text_lower[j].isalnum():
            j += 1
            
        word = text_lower[i:j]
        if word == username_lower:
            return True
        i = j
    
    return False

def solve_chat(users, messages):
    # Create a copy of messages
    message_copy = []
    for msg in messages:
        parts = msg.split(':', 1)
        sender = parts[0]
        text = parts[1]
        message_copy.append((sender, text))
    
    # Check known sender constraints
    for i in range(len(message_copy)):
        sender, text = message_copy[i]
        if sender == '?':
            continue
            
        # Check if sender mentions himself
        if is_word_mentioned(text, sender):
            return "Impossible"
        
        # Check consecutive messages
        if i > 0 and message_copy[i-1][0] == sender:
            return "Impossible"
    
    # Try to assign senders to unknown messages
    for i in range(len(message_copy)):
        if message_copy[i][0] == '?':
            possible_senders = []
            
            for user in users:
                user_lower = user.lower()
                
                # Check if this user mentions himself
                if is_word_mentioned(message_copy[i][1], user):
                    continue
                
                # Check constraints with neighbors
                valid = True
                
                # Check left neighbor
                if i > 0:
                    left_sender = message_copy[i-1][0]
                    if left_sender != '?' and left_sender.lower() == user.lower():
                        valid = False
                
                # Check right neighbor
                if i < len(message_copy) - 1:
                    right_sender = message_copy[i+1][0]
                    if right_sender != '?' and right_sender.lower() == user.lower():
                        valid = False
                
                if valid:
                    possible_senders.append(user)
            
            if not possible_senders:
                return "Impossible"
            
            message_copy[i] = (possible_senders[0], message_copy[i][1])
    
    # Final validation
    for i in range(len(message_copy)):
        sender, text = message_copy[i]
        if sender == '?':
            continue
            
        # Check if sender mentions himself
        if is_word_mentioned(text, sender):
            return "Impossible"
            
        # Check consecutive messages
        if i > 0 and message_copy[i-1][0] == sender:
            return "Impossible"
            
        if i < len(message_copy) - 1 and message_copy[i+1][0] == sender:
            return "Impossible"
    
    return message_copy

def solve():
    t = int(input())
    
    for _ in range(t):
        n = int(input())
        users = input().split()
        
        m = int(input())
        messages = []
        for _ in range(m):
            messages.append(input().strip())

            print("Impossible")

                print(f"{msg[0]}:{msg[1]}")
```

def is_word_mentioned(text, username):
    """Check if username appears as a word in text"""
    text_lower = text.lower()
    username_lower = username.lower()
    
    # Find all word boundaries
    i = 0
    while i < len(text_lower):
        # Skip non-alphanumeric characters
        while i < len(text_lower) and not text_lower[i].isalnum():
            i += 1
        if i >= len(text_lower):
            break
            
        # Find end of word
        j = i
        while j < len(text_lower) and text_lower[j].isalnum():
            j += 1
            
        word = text_lower[i:j]
        if word == username_lower:
            return True
        i = j
    
    return False

def solve_chat(users, messages):
    # Create a copy of messages
    message_copy = []
    for msg in messages:
        parts = msg.split(':', 1)
        sender = parts[0]
        text = parts[1]
        message_copy.append((sender, text))
    
    # Check known sender constraints
    for i in range(len(message_copy)):
        sender, text = message_copy[i]
        if sender == '?':
            continue
            
        # Check if sender mentions himself
        if is_word_mentioned(text, sender):
            return "Impossible"
        
        # Check consecutive messages
        if i > 0 and message_copy[i-1][0] == sender:
            return "Impossible"
    
    # Try to assign senders to unknown messages
    for i in range(len(message_copy)):
        if message_copy[i][0] == '?':
            possible_senders = []
            
            for user in users:
                user_lower = user.lower()
                
                # Check if this user mentions himself
                if is_word_mentioned(message_copy[i][1], user):
                    continue
                
                # Check constraints with neighbors
                valid = True
                
                # Check left neighbor
                if i > 0:
                    left_sender = message_copy[i-1][0]
                    if left_sender != '?' and left_sender.lower() == user.lower():
                        valid = False
                
                # Check right neighbor
                if i < len(message_copy) - 1:
                    right_sender = message_copy[i+1][0]
                    if right_sender != '?' and right_sender.lower() == user.lower():
                        valid = False
                
                if valid:
                    possible_senders.append(user)
            
            if not possible_senders:
                return "Impossible"
            
            message_copy[i] = (possible_senders[0], message_copy[i][1])
    
    # Final validation
    for i in range(len(message_copy)):
        sender, text = message_copy[i]
        if sender == '?':
            continue
            
        # Check if sender mentions himself
        if is_word_mentioned(text, sender):
            return "Impossible"
            
        # Check consecutive messages
        if i > 0 and message_copy[i-1][0] == sender:
            return "Impossible"
            
        if i < len(message_copy) - 1 and message_copy[i+1][0] == sender:
            return "Impossible"
    
    return message_copy

def solve():
    t = int(input())
    
    for _ in range(t):
        n = int(input())
        users = input().split()
        
        m = int(input())
        messages = []
        for _ in range(m):
            messages.append(input().strip())
        
        result = solve_chat(users, messages)
        
        if result == "Impossible":
            print("Impossible")
        else:
            for msg in result:
                print(f"{msg[0]}:{msg[1]}")

solve()

View Problem Statement 
You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

Recently Vladik discovered a new entertainment — coding bots for social networks. He would like to use machine learning in his bots so now he want to prepare some learning data for them.

At first, he need to download t chats. Vladik coded a script which should have downloaded the chats, however, something went wrong. In particular, some of the messages have no information of their sender. It is known that if a person sends several messages in a row, they all are merged into a single message. It means that there could not be two or more messages in a row with the same sender. Moreover, a sender never mention himself in his messages.

Vladik wants to recover senders of all the messages so that each two neighboring messages will have different senders and no sender will mention himself in his messages.

He has no idea of how to do this, and asks you for help. Help Vladik to recover senders in each of the chats!


-----Input-----

The first line contains single integer t (1 ≤ t ≤ 10) — the number of chats. The t chats follow. Each chat is given in the following format.

The first line of each chat description contains single integer n (1 ≤ n ≤ 100) — the number of users in the chat.

The next line contains n space-separated distinct usernames. Each username consists of lowercase and uppercase English letters and digits. The usernames can't start with a digit. Two usernames are different even if they differ only with letters' case. The length of username is positive and doesn't exceed 10 characters.

The next line contains single integer m (1 ≤ m ≤ 100) — the number of messages in the chat. The next m line contain the messages in the following formats, one per line:   <username>:<text> — the format of a message with known sender. The username should appear in the list of usernames of the chat.  <?>:<text> — the format of a message with unknown sender. 

The text of a message can consist of lowercase and uppercase English letter, digits, characters '.' (dot), ',' (comma), '!' (exclamation mark), '?' (question mark) and ' ' (space). The text doesn't contain trailing spaces. The length of the text is positive and doesn't exceed 100 characters.

We say that a text mention a user if his username appears in the text as a word. In other words, the username appears in a such a position that the two characters before and after its appearance either do not exist or are not English letters or digits. For example, the text "Vasya, masha13 and Kate!" can mention users "Vasya", "masha13", "and" and "Kate", but not "masha".

It is guaranteed that in each chat no known sender mention himself in his messages and there are no two neighboring messages with the same known sender.


-----Output-----

Print the information about the t chats in the following format:

If it is not possible to recover senders, print single line "Impossible" for this chat. Otherwise print m messages in the following format:

<username>:<text>

If there are multiple answers, print any of them.

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have t chats, each with n users and m messages
2. Some messages have known senders, some have unknown senders (marked with "?")
3. Constraints:
   - No two adjacent messages can have the same sender
   - A sender cannot mention themselves in their message
   - If a person sends multiple messages in a row, they're merged into one message (so adjacent messages must have different senders)

## Key Insights

1. For unknown messages (marked with "?"), we need to assign senders such that:
   - The sender is different from the previous and next messages (if they exist)
   - The sender doesn't appear in the message text (as a word)

2. We need to check if it's possible to assign senders to all messages.

3. The approach:
   - Start with known senders and process messages sequentially
   - For each unknown message, try to assign a valid sender
   - A valid sender must:
     - Not be the same as previous message's sender
     - Not be the same as next message's sender (if next exists)
     - Not appear in the message text as a word
   - If no valid assignment exists at any point, return "Impossible"

## Step-by-Step Solution

1. Parse input to get users, messages, and their text
2. For each message, check if it's known or unknown
3. For unknown messages, find valid senders by checking constraints
4. Use backtracking or greedy approach - since constraints are small, we can try simple greedy assignment
5. Verify all constraints are satisfied

Let me implement this:

```python
def extract_mentions(text, users):
    """Extract all usernames that appear as complete words in text"""
    mentions = set()
    words = []
    i = 0
    while i < len(text):
        # Find word boundaries
        if text[i].isalnum():
            start = i
            while i < len(text) and text[i].isalnum():
                i += 1
            word = text[start:i]
            words.append(word)
        else:
            i += 1
    
    # Check if any word matches a user (case-sensitive)
    for word in words:
        if word in users:
            mentions.add(word)
    
    return mentions

def can_assign_senders(messages, users, known_senders):
    """Check if we can assign senders to all messages"""
    n = len(messages)
    if n == 0:
        return True
    
    # Create a list to store the final sender for each message
    senders = [-1] * n  # -1 means unknown
    
    # Initialize known senders
    for i, msg in enumerate(messages):
        if msg.startswith("?:"):
            senders[i] = -1
        else:
            # Extract sender and text
            sender_part = msg.split(":", 1)[0]
            senders[i] = sender_part
    
    # Process messages sequentially
    for i in range(n):
        if senders[i] == -1:  # Unknown sender
            # Find valid sender candidates
            valid_senders = []
            for user in users:
                # Check constraints
                is_valid = True
                
                # Cannot be the same as previous message
                if i > 0 and senders[i-1] == user:
                    is_valid = False
                
                # Cannot be the same as next message (if exists)
                if i < n-1 and senders[i+1] == user:
                    is_valid = False
                
                # Cannot mention themselves in message
                text = messages[i].split(":", 1)[1]
                if user in extract_mentions(text, users):
                    is_valid = False
                
                if is_valid:
                    valid_senders.append(user)
            
            # If no valid sender found, impossible
            if not valid_senders:
                return False
            
            # Assign any valid sender (greedy choice)
            senders[i] = valid_senders[0]
    
    # Final check: adjacent messages have different senders
    for i in range(n-1):
        if senders[i] == senders[i+1]:
            return False
    
    return True

def solve_chat(n, users, messages):
    # Try to assign senders
    if can_assign_senders(messages, users, []):
        result = []
        for msg in messages:
            if msg.startswith("?:"):
                # Find a valid sender
                sender = None
                for user in users:
                    # Check constraints
                    is_valid = True
                    
                    # Cannot be same as previous
                    if len(result) > 0 and result[-1] == user:
                        is_valid = False
                    
                    # Cannot be same as next (if exists)
                    next_idx = len(result)
                    if next_idx < len(messages) - 1 and messages[next_idx + 1].startswith("?:"):
                        # We need to check the next message that will be assigned
                        # This is complex, so we'll do it differently
                        pass
                
                # Let's do a better approach: try to assign in order
                pass
        
        # Actually, let's implement the simple greedy assignment
        result = []
        for msg in messages:
            if msg.startswith("?:"):
                # Find valid sender
                valid_senders = []
                for user in users:
                    is_valid = True
                    
                    # Check against previous message
                    if len(result) > 0 and result[-1] == user:
                        is_valid = False
                    
                    # Check against next message (if exists)
                    next_msg_idx = len(result)
                    if next_msg_idx < len(messages) - 1:
                        next_msg = messages[next_msg_idx + 1]
                        if next_msg.startswith("?:"):
                            # We don't know the next sender yet, so we need to check if user could be assigned
                            # This is tricky - we need to try all possibilities
                            pass
                        else:
                            # Next message has known sender
                            if user == next_msg.split(":", 1)[0]:
                                is_valid = False
                    else:
                        # No next message
                        pass
                    
                    # Check if user mentions themselves
                    text = msg.split(":", 1)[1]
                    if user in extract_mentions(text, users):
                        is_valid = False
                    
                    if is_valid:
                        valid_senders.append(user)
                
                if not valid_senders:
                    return "Impossible"
                
                result.append(valid_senders[0] + ":" + msg.split(":", 1)[1])
            else:
                result.append(msg)
        
        return result
    else:
        return "Impossible"

def main():
    t = int(input())
    
    for _ in range(t):
        n = int(input())
        users = input().split()
        m = int(input())
        messages = []
        for _ in range(m):
            messages.append(input())
        
        # Simple greedy approach for assigning unknown senders
        result = []
        for msg in messages:
            if msg.startswith("?:"):
                # Try to find a valid sender
                valid_senders = []
                for user in users:
                    is_valid = True
                    
                    # Check against previous message
                    if len(result) > 0 and result[-1] == user:
                        is_valid = False
                    
                    # Check against next message (if exists)
                    next_idx = len(result)
                    if next_idx < len(messages) - 1:
                        next_msg = messages[next_idx + 1]
                        next_sender = next_msg.split(":", 1)[0] if not next_msg.startswith("?:") else None
                        if next_sender is not None and next_sender == user:
                            is_valid = False
                    
                    # Check if user appears in message text
                    text = msg.split(":", 1)[1]
                    if user in extract_mentions(text, users):
                        is_valid = False
                    
                    if is_valid:
                        valid_senders.append(user)
                
                if not valid_senders:
                    print("Impossible")
                    break
                
                result.append(valid_senders[0] + ":" + msg.split(":", 1)[1])
            else:
                result.append(msg)
        
        else:  # Only if we didn't break
            print("\n".join(result))

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have t chats, each with n users and m messages
2. Some messages have known senders, some have unknown senders (marked as "?")
3. Constraints:
   - No two consecutive messages can have the same sender
   - A sender cannot mention themselves in their message
   - If a person sends multiple messages in a row, they're merged into one message (so no consecutive same senders)
4. We need to assign senders to all "?" messages satisfying the constraints

## Step-by-Step Solution

### Step 1: Parse the input and understand the constraints

For each chat:
- We have n users (given as a list)
- We have m messages with either known sender or unknown sender ("?")
- For each message, we need to check if the sender mentions themselves

### Step 2: Algorithm approach

This is a constraint satisfaction problem. We need to:
1. First check if any known sender mentions themselves (already guaranteed by input)
2. For messages with "?", we need to assign senders such that:
   - Adjacent messages have different senders
   - No sender mentions themselves in their message
3. We can use backtracking or greedy assignment

### Step 3: Key insights

- For consecutive messages, the sender must be different
- For each unknown message, we need to check which usernames are valid (don't mention themselves)
- We can try to assign senders greedily when possible

### Step 4: Implementation strategy

1. Parse the input
2. For each message with "?", determine valid senders (those not mentioned in the text)
3. Check constraints:
   - No consecutive same sender
   - No self-mention
4. Try to assign senders using a greedy approach or simple constraint checking

Let me implement this:

```python
def get_mentions(text, usernames):
    """Extract all word mentions from text"""
    mentions = set()
    words = []
    i = 0
    while i < len(text):
        if text[i].isalnum():
            start = i
            while i < len(text) and text[i].isalnum():
                i += 1
            word = text[start:i]
            words.append(word)
        else:
            i += 1
    
    # Check which words are in usernames (case-sensitive)
    for word in words:
        if word in usernames:
            mentions.add(word)
    
    return mentions

def solve_chat():
    n = int(input())
    usernames = input().split()
    m = int(input())
    
    messages = []
    for _ in range(m):
        line = input().strip()
        parts = line.split(':', 1)
        sender = parts[0]
        text = parts[1] if len(parts) > 1 else ""
        messages.append((sender, text))
    
    # Check if any known sender mentions themselves
    for sender, text in messages:
        if sender != "?" and sender in get_mentions(text, usernames):
            return "Impossible"
    
    # For messages with ?, we need to assign senders
    # We'll use a greedy approach: for each message with ?, 
    # pick a sender that is different from the previous message and doesn't mention themselves
    
    result = []
    prev_sender = None
    
    for sender, text in messages:
        if sender != "?":
            # Known sender - check constraints
            if prev_sender is not None and sender == prev_sender:
                return "Impossible"
            result.append((sender, text))
            prev_sender = sender
        else:
            # Unknown sender - find valid senders
            valid_senders = []
            for u in usernames:
                # Check if this username is mentioned in the text
                if u not in get_mentions(text, usernames):
                    valid_senders.append(u)
            
            # If no valid sender, impossible
            if not valid_senders:
                return "Impossible"
            
            # Choose a sender that is different from the previous one
            chosen_sender = None
            for u in valid_senders:
                if prev_sender is None or u != prev_sender:
                    chosen_sender = u
                    break
            
            if chosen_sender is None:
                # All valid senders are the same as previous - impossible
                return "Impossible"
            
            result.append((chosen_sender, text))
            prev_sender = chosen_sender
    
    return result

# Main execution
t = int(input())
for _ in range(t):
    result = solve_chat()
    if result == "Impossible":
        print("Impossible")
    else:
        for sender, text in result:
            print(f"{sender}:{text}")
```

Wait, I need to check the examples again to make sure this works:

Example 1:
- Users: Vladik, netman
- Messages: "? : Hello, Vladik!", "? : Hi"
- For first message: valid senders are Vladik, netman. Vladik appears in text, so netman is only valid. Assign netman.
- For second message: valid senders are Vladik, netman. Previous was netman, so Vladik is chosen. 
- Check: Vladik doesn't mention himself in "Hi" ✓

This matches expected output.

Example 2:
- Users: netman, vladik
- Messages: "netman:how are you?", "? : wrong message", "vladik:im fine"
- First message: netman is valid, and doesn't mention himself
- Second message: text is "wrong message" - mentions nothing, so both netman and vladik are valid
- Third message: vladik is valid (doesn't mention himself)
- Previous to third message is vladik, so second message cannot be vladik
- So second message must be netman
- Check: netman in "wrong message"? No (netman not in "wrong message")
- So netman is valid
- But now we have: netman → netman → vladik
- Wait, consecutive same sender: netman then netman - this violates constraint!

In example 2, the second message has sender netman, same as first message. This violates "no two consecutive messages with same sender".

So the algorithm should catch this when assigning the second message.

In my current code, I check consecutive senders - so when assigning the second message, I check if it equals the previous (netman). Since the previous was netman, any sender assigned must not be netman. But netman is valid (not mentioned in text), and vladik is also valid.

So I need to pick vladik for second message. Then third is vladik - same as second? No, third is vladik, second is vladik - that's consecutive same sender!

I need to modify - when assigning second message, I need to ensure it's different from first (netman), so only vladik is possible. 

Then third message must be different from second, so can't be vladik. But third is vladik - this is a conflict.

So the assignment should fail - which matches "Impossible".

Let me fix the code:

```python
def get_mentions(text, usernames):
    """Extract all word mentions from text"""
    mentions = set()
    words = []
    i = 0
    while i < len(text):
        if text[i].isalnum():
            start = i
            while i < len(text) and text[i].isalnum():
                i += 1
            word = text[start:i]
            words.append(word)
        else:
            i += 1
    
    # Check which words are in usernames (case-sensitive)
    for word in words:
        if word in usernames:
            mentions.add(word)
    
    return mentions

def solve_chat():
    n = int(input())
    usernames = input().split()
    m = int(input())
    
    messages = []
    for _ in range(m):
        line = input().strip()
        parts = line.split(':', 1)
        sender = parts[0]
        text = parts[1] if len(parts) > 1 else ""
        messages.append((sender, text))
    
    # Check if any known sender mentions themselves
    for sender, text in messages:
        if sender != "?" and sender in get_mentions(text, usernames):
            return "Impossible"
    
    # For messages with ?, we need to assign senders
    result = []
    prev_sender = None
    
    for sender, text in messages:
        if sender != "?":
            # Known sender - check constraints
            if prev_sender is not None and sender == prev_sender:
                return "Impossible"
            result.append((sender, text))
            prev_sender = sender
        else:
            # Unknown sender - find valid senders
            valid_senders = []
            for u in usernames:
                # Check if this username is mentioned in the text
                if u not in get_mentions(text, usernames):
                    valid_senders.append(u)
            
            # If no valid sender, impossible
            if not valid_senders:
                return "Impossible"
            
            # Choose a sender that is different from the previous one
            chosen_sender = None
            for u in valid_senders:
                if prev_sender is None or u != prev_sender:
                    chosen_sender = u
                    break
            
            if chosen_sender is None:
                # All valid senders are the same as previous - impossible
                return "Impossible"
            
            result.append((chosen_sender, text))
            prev_sender = chosen_sender
    
    return result

# Main execution
```

def get_mentions(text, usernames):
    """Extract all word mentions from text"""
    mentions = set()
    words = []
    i = 0
    while i < len(text):
        if text[i].isalnum():
            start = i
            while i < len(text) and text[i].isalnum():
                i += 1
            word = text[start:i]
            words.append(word)
        else:
            i += 1
    
    # Check which words are in usernames (case-sensitive)
    for word in words:
        if word in usernames:
            mentions.add(word)
    
    return mentions

def solve_chat():
    n = int(input())
    usernames = input().split()
    m = int(input())
    
    messages = []
    for _ in range(m):
        line = input().strip()
        parts = line.split(':', 1)
        sender = parts[0]
        text = parts[1] if len(parts) > 1 else ""
        messages.append((sender, text))
    
    # Check if any known sender mentions themselves
    for sender, text in messages:
        if sender != "?" and sender in get_mentions(text, usernames):
            return "Impossible"
    
    # For messages with ?, we need to assign senders
    result = []
    prev_sender = None
    
    for sender, text in messages:
        if sender != "?":
            # Known sender - check constraints
            if prev_sender is not None and sender == prev_sender:
                return "Impossible"
            result.append((sender, text))
            prev_sender = sender
        else:
            # Unknown sender - find valid senders
            valid_senders = []
            for u in usernames:
                # Check if this username is mentioned in the text
                if u not in get_mentions(text, usernames):
                    valid_senders.append(u)
            
            # If no valid sender, impossible
            if not valid_senders:
                return "Impossible"
            
            # Choose a sender that is different from the previous one
            chosen_sender = None
            for u in valid_senders:
                if prev_sender is None or u != prev_sender:
                    chosen_sender = u
                    break
            
            if chosen_sender is None:
                # All valid senders are the same as previous - impossible
                return "Impossible"
            
            result.append((chosen_sender, text))
            prev_sender = chosen_sender
    
    return result

# Main execution

        print("Impossible")
